LD ARTILLERY FIRE DIRECTION SYSTEM

TAi                               PE RATION

PURPOÉ

     This manual provides information and guidance for field artillery (FA)
personnel, especially commanders and their staffs, on the tactical employment
of the tactical fire dirección system (TACFIRE).

SCOPE

     Techniques and procedures, for employment; command, control, and
communication; fire support planning; fire support coordination; and training
with TACFIRE are described.

     This manual should be used with FM 100-5; FM 6-20; FM 6-20-1; FM
6-20-2; other field manuals in the 6- series; and with TACFIRE user manuals
that explain the technical operations oreequipment parts.

     The information in this manual applies to all types of warfare unless
otherwise stated.

TARGET ÂUOOEÊ^CE

  This manual is for fire support system managers—field artillery officers
(commanders, FSOs, FDOs, etc.) and FA noncommissioned officers in the
speciality areas of field artillery operations/intelligepce, targeting, and survey
(MOS 13C/13E/13F/17C/82C).

Section II. DIRECT SUPPORT
FA BATTALION OPERATIONS

  A direct support field artillery battalion       command and control system. The principal
gives responsive field artillery fires in          function of the division artillery system is to
support of a maneuver brigade. The battalion       provide efficient, effective, and timely
operations element is the staff agency that        command and control to all organic and
gives the control necessary to use available       supporting field artillery resources. The
field artillery resources effectively to destroy,  battalion operations center of the direct
neutralize, or suppress surface targets            support field artillery battalion provides
jeopardizing the accomplishment of the             command and control to field artillery
brigade mission.                                   resources in support ofthe maneuver brigade.

  The tactical fire direction system gives the      2-11. RELATIONSHIP WITH
battalion operations center an automated            OTHER ORGANIZATIONS
system for command and control. TACFIRE            AND ELEMENTS
increases the effectiveness of the field
artillery support to the brigade without           Division Artillery
materially affecting the command
relationships or the doctrine and tactics             The operations center of the direct support
governing employment of the direct support         battalion accesses the conventional and
battalion. This increased effectiveness            nuclear analysis and the fallout prediction
results from the use oftactical automatic data     capabilities of the div arty TACFIRE
processing to achieve more comprehensive           computer to support the fire support elements
and timely analysis of fire support abilities,     with the maneuver units. Additionally, the
more efficient allocation of fire, and             div arty computer system tactical data files
increased fire support responsiveness.             contain operational, intelligence, and
TACFIRE also enables the battalion                 technical data. The battalion operations
operations center to control more than three       center may also access these files for
batteries effectively.                             information required to support current and
                                                   future operations.

  o. The problem. During a div arty
command post exercise (CPX), while the CF
VFMED was down for maintenance, MAJ
Richardson directed SFC Franks, the
artillery control console operator, to modify
the current standard values in the artillery
target intelligence program by values which
he (MAJ R) provided, to allow a greater
number of target combinations. MAJ
Richardson determined that SFC Franks
applied the modification values incorrectly.
This fact and the time taken to complete the
task convinced MAJ Richardson that SFC
Franks had difficulty in modifying the ATI
criteria.

i     " •        —i

  b. Analyze. Recognizing certain tasks

that SFC Franks could not do was the start of
the analysis process. Because MAJ
Richardson is completely knowledgeable of

the skills required of his ACCO, his analysis
was based primarily upon his observations of
the operator's performance. If MAJ
Richardson had not known these required
skills, he would have relied upon some
objective measure of proficiency, such as
evaluation under the conditions and to the
standards listed in the soldier's manual.
Having identified the problem, MAJ
Richardson's training objective is that SFC
Franks know how to modify the criteria
values in the ATI program correctly.

extension training materials. The training

needs and recommends the most efficient

                              IN TACFIRE

Aimpoint        A point on the ground where the employment of a nuclear weapon(s)
                will achieve the desired effects against the enemy without violating
Available sup-  the commander's guidance.
ply rate (ASR)
Auxiliary       TACFIRE makes use of the mnemonic ASR for what is currently
memory          defined as controlled supply rate (CSR). See CSR.
Bit
                The memory or storage provided by a device other than the computer,
Catastrophic    In TACFIRE, the ARMM is an auxiliary memory device.
failure
Circular        1. Abbreviation for binary digit. Binary digits are 0 and 1.
access          2. Device or media for storing a binary digit. In TACFIRE the
Commander's     computer word length is 32 bits plus a parity bit for a total of 33 bits all
criteria        stored on magnetic cores.

                An equipment failure that precludes further operation of a subsystem.

                Method of retrieving survey control points by input of a center
                coordinate and radius to the survey-application function.

                Standing processing guidelines automatically considered by
                TACFIRE in the preparation of recommendations. Examples are
                ammunition restrictions and volume of fire criteria.

                                                                               FM 6-1

Commonality Having common features and characteristics.

Compute      Associates fire request with unit(s), target, and time to fire. Used in
             nonnuclear fire planning to indicate the computer generations of the
Computer     schedule of fires.

Computer     A TACFIRE computer consists of three main elements: the central
center       processing unit (CPU), the input/output unit (IOU), and the memory.

Computer     The equipment that is the nucleus of the computerized operation,
group        At battalion level, all equipment is housed in one shelter, which
Coordinated  includes the computer group and display group. At division artillery
fire line    level, two shelters house the computer group and display group
(CFL)        separately.

Continuity   The group of equipment including the computer, auxiliary storage
of           devices, and communication equipment.
operations
             A coordinated fire line is a line beyond which conventional fire support
             means (FA, NGF, and mortars) may fire at any time within the zone of
             the establishing headquarters without additional need for coordi-
             nation. It is designated to expedite fires across boundaries and to
             enhance rapid fire support reactions to targets in those areas. In
             TACFIRE the coordinated fire line is specified by use of the NFL
             mnemonic. See NFL.

             Continuous command and control provided by a mutual support
             unit during normal displacements of a computer center or failure
             of a computer center.

Controlled   The number of rounds per tube per day per weapon system made
supply rate  available by the commander for expenditure for a specific time
(CSR)        period. (Previously known as ASR. ASR is used by TACFIRE.)
             See ASR.
Core memory
             A storage unit composed of magnetic cores and associated wiring.
Data base    Each core stores a single bit.

Data         The complete dynamic and static data required for subsystem
management   operation.

             Processing accomplished by an operating system to provide
             storage space and data to computer programs.

                                                                                       D-7


---

FM 6-1

Data security  Measures taken to preclude unauthorized access to data or files
               by a program.

Desired ground TACFIRE makes use of the mnemonic DGZ for what is currently

zero (DGZ)     defined as aimpoint. See aimpoint.

Digital data   Data represented by binary digits. In digital transmission, a 1200-
               hertz tone represents 1 and a 2400-hertz tone represents 0. See
               frequency shift keying.

Digital        An interface where digital information flow takes place,
interface

Display group The group of equipment including the ACC, DPM, ETD, and ELP.

Duplex         A term applied to a circuit that makes possible the simultaneous
               transmission of two messages in opposite directions.

Dynamic data See operational data.

Dynamic        The ability to allocate or store varying amounts of data based on
storage        varying requirements.
Echelon
               One of the different levels of a command.
Execute
               Used in nuclear and nonnuclear fire planning to indicate the computer
Fault          generation desired of the ballistic solutions for the plan.
detection
Fault          The determination that a faulty condition exists in equipment.
isolation
               The location of a fault in equipment to the extent that repair is
File           possible.
maintenance
               Since the TACFIRE computer memories are not infinite, it is possible
Fire           to inundate the memories to a point where system functions become
coordination   degraded. File maintenance is the process of validating computer
area (FCA)     data to insure only accurate and current information is stored in
               the computer.

               An area in which specific restraints have been imposed and into
               which fires in excess of those restraints will not be delivered without
               approval of the establishing authority. The FCA is a closed area
               specified by a minimum of three points, or a point and radius, the
               name of the agency establishing the area, and the name of the area.

              The TACFIRE FCA is used to designate free fire area (FFA),
              restrictive fire area (RFA), and no fire area (NFA).

Fire          TACFIRE makes use of the mnemonic FCL for what is currently
coordination
line (FCL)    defined as restrictive fire line (RFL). FCL will appear on all TACFIRE
Fire support
coordination  geometry and output reports instead of RFL. See RFL.  , v             .
line (FSCL)
              The fire support coordination line is a line beyond which all targets
Fire unit     may be attacked by any weapon system (including aircraft and special
              weapons) without endangering troops or requiring additional
Free fire     coordination with the establishing headquarters. This expedites
area (FFA)    the attack of targets. The FSCL is established by the force
              commander (normally corps).
Frequency
shift keying  The basic unit (battery) used in TACFIRE for status reporting such
              as location, ammunition, etc.
Frontline
trace (FRIT)  Free fire area is an area into which any fire support means may
              deliver fires without additional coordination. This speeds reaction
GB            to targets in the FFA. It can be used for an area where neutralization
Geometry      by fire support is preferred to the use of maneuver forces or where
Hardware      friendly aircraft can jettison ammunition. TACFIRE makes use of
Hertz         the mnemonic FCA for what is currently defined as the free fire area
              (FFA). See FCA. (Using a TACFIRE FCA to define an FFA can inhibit
              chemical target analysis by FSE.)

              A method of transmitting the mark and space elements of a data code
              by shifting the carrier frequency a fixed amount. The TACFIRE
              DDT (RDT) will convert the digital data information which is in the
              form of I's and 0's to a usable medium for wire transmission or
              modulation for radio transmission.

              It is the most forward location of friendly maneuver forces on line,
              specified by a minimum of two points. The line is designated from
              left to right, facing the enemy. The TACFIRE FRLT is used to
              designate the forward edge of the battle area (FEBA).

              Chemical nerve agent.

              Control lines, boundaries, and areas used to coordinate fire and
              and maneuver.

              General term applied to equipment and equipment components.

Initialization  The procedure for preparing a TACFIRE subsystem for operation.
Interrupt       Includes turning on power, equipment checks, and input ofoperational
K               data into the system.
Light pen
Link            A signal that creates computer program intervention and refers
Magnetic tape   the computer to another operation.
Map module
                K is a term used to abbreviate large numbers associated with memory
                or auxiliary memory capabilities. The value of IK is 1024 which is 210.
                Examples are: 32K = 32 * 1024 = 32,768; and 64K = 64* 1024 = 65,536.

                A device held against a point on the ETD to indicate data for
                processing.

                General term used to indicate the existence of communication
                facilities between two points.

                A tape or ribbon of any material impregnated or coated with
                magnetic or other material on which information may be placed
                in the form of magnetically polarized spots.

                The computer program that maintains coordinates defining the
                general area of operations. It provides high order digits, grid zone,
                and spheroid to form complete coordinates.

Memory          The core memory of the computer.

Memory word The TACFIRE memory word consists of 32 bits plus a parity bit for
                     a total of 33 bits.

Message         The comparison of input message entries with stored file entries to
validation      confirm that the input is meaningful. For example, a message to
                update the status of a specific fire unit is meaningful only if that fire

                unit is in the AFU file.

Modularity      Constructed in modules.

Mutual support  The term applied to the techniques employed to continuously
                update a computer with information from another computer so that
                the first may assume the duties of the second should the second
                computer fail or displace and vice versa.

No fire area    The no fire area is a designated area into which neither fires nor
(NFA)           effects from fires will occur. Normally, the NFA is established
                by a division or corps commander. TACFIRE makes use of the

              FM 6-1

No fire line  mnemonic FCA for what is currently defined as no fire area (NFA).
(NFL)         See FCA.

              TACFIRE makes use of the mnemonic NFL for what is currently
              defined as coordinated fire line (CFL). NFL will appear on all
              TACFIRE geometry and output reports instead of CFL. See CFL.

Operational   Data input to TACFIRE during initialization or normal operations.
data
Ordering      Placing items in a particular sequence. For example, ordering of
              fire units is accomplished considering weighting factors established
Parity bit    as commander's criteria.

Parity error  A bit in a group of bits that is set so that the total number of 1-bits
              is either even or odd. The parity bit in a TACFIRE memory word is
Peripheral    set so that the number of l-bits is odd.
equipment
Priority      The error detected when a sum of l-bits does not agree with the parity
scheme        bit indication of an even or odd total.

Program       Equipment that is attached to and/or communicates with a computer,
security      TACFIRE peripherals include the ACC, DPM, printer, and DDT.
Program
sequence      The overall definition of processing priorities to programs and
Queue         messages. For example, a fire mission has high priority in TACFIRE
              and is processed before lower priority functions. Individual message
Rectangular   priorities can be set by the operator.
access
Remote        Measures taken to preclude alteration of a program as a result of
station       normal processing.

              A sequence in which programs must be performed to accomplish
              a specified task.

              A waiting line. In TACFIRE, messages are placed in a queue to wait
              for processing in accordance with the priority scheme.

              Method of retrieving survey control points by input of two comer
              coordinates and a width to the survey application.

              Input or remote equipment operated from a location outside a

---

FM 6-1

Response time The tíme from receipt of initial fire commands or a warning order at
                     the fire unit until ready to fire is reported.

Restrictive    The restrictive fire area establishes constraints on fire support.
fire area .    Fires in excess of these constraints must be cleared with the establish-
(RFA)          ing headquarters (battalion or higher)!. Thé RFA applies to con-
               ventional ammunition outside the constraints imposed. TACFIRE
               makes use of the mnemonic FCA for what is currently defined as
               restrictive fire area (RFA). See FCA.

Restrictive    A restrictive fire line is a line established between two friendly
fire line      forces and across which fires and their effects cannot extend without
(RFL)          prior coordination with the affected force. The RFL is established by
               the commander of both forces and applies to all types of ammunition
               and fires. In TACFIRE the restrictive fire line is specified by use of
               the FCL mnemonic. See FCL.

Salvage point  Periodic recording of operational data on the ARMM for use in
recording      system recovery.

Simplex        The operational characteristics of a circuit that permit communi-
               cations in only one direction at a time; half duplex.

Software       General term applied to computer programs.

Static data    Data placed on the system tape used to initialize TACFIRE computer
               centers. The static data on the system tape cannot be altered in the
               field. Except for a few special cases, static data once initialized
               cannot be altered.

Subsystem      The TACFIRE equipment, programs, and personnel that are
TACFIRE        associated with a computer center. Remote stations are included in
               subsystems.

System         Resumption of operation with all equipment after degraded mode
recovery       operation due to an equipment failure.

System         The TACFIRE equipment, programs, and personnel that support
(TACFIRE)      the command and control structure throughout an organization.
               A system is made up of two or more subsystems.

System tape    The tape (tape transport cartridge) that contains programs and static
               data base used to initialize TACFIRE subsystems.

                                                                                   FM 6-1

Tape transport   A sealed cartridge containing magnetic tape, a, drive motor, and
cartridge (TTC)  read/write circuitry. This cartridge is plugged into the AEMM
                 when in use.

Task             Processing necessary to accomplish a function. A task may be

                 interrupted and restarted and may require processing from several

                 program modules.

Task priority The priority of a task in a group of tasks.

Task             The scheduling of a group of tasks so that all tasks will be
scheduling       accomplished in accordance with the priority scheme.

Thrust access    Method of retrieving survey control points by input of two end point
                 coordinates for a line and distances either side of that line to the
                 survey application function.

User             Commands that permit operators to transmit, edit, delete, and
commands         print files and file entries.

VX               Chemical nerve agent.
